                      '.OOWNSVILLE, QUEENSLAND, ~810.



                      Dear Sir,
                                 I acknowledge your letter HQTVL 5/40/Air(83) of 16th September
                      reporting an U.F.O. seen and heard near Bang Bang Homestead on 14th
                      September.

       -<.                       The report s~ems highly compatible with a meteorite entering the
                      earth's atmosphere, becoming incandescent and ' then disinte grating explosively.
                     lft could be very helpful if the questionaires being collected by t he Cbncurry
                      Polioe were to yield sufficiently precise information on speeds, heights and
                      directions to enable search parties to locate meteorite fraement a in t he
                      localit,r. I am sure that Queensland scientific bodies would be very grateful
                      to receive from ~ou any such information you can collect and col l ate.

        ~-                      I have verified that Weapons Research Establishment i s unaware of any
                      rockets being fired in or towards North Queensland on that date. I am
                      awaiting reoeipt within a few days of the fortnightly bulletin publi shed by
                      Goddard Space Flight Centre, Greenbelt, Maryland, U.S.A., on behalf of the
                      I.U.W.D.s. World Warning Agenoy for Satellites.   The next issue (published
                      to-day) will detail ~ satellites known to have re-ente~ed the atmosphere
                      in the period 9th to 22nd September. I shall ~te again to inform you if it
                      transpires that any artificial satellite decay . on 14th September •
      ....                      The report of further explosions heard in the same area. 16 hours
                      later seems, at first glance, rather unlikely. Confirmation from additional
                      witnesses seems essential before accepting the reality of such an improbable
                      coincidence.

                                I shall be glad to hear further news of this event from lOU if
                      learn anything significant from later reports.




                                                                            (Brian Harkin)
                                                                    (for) PRINCIPAL OFFICER
                                                                        SPACE RESEARCH GROUP.

                       REPORT OF UFO NORTH QUEENSLAND 14TH SEPI'EMBER 19()9


               This headquarters bas received reports of a bright l y glowing
     object with a tail of smoke s ighted near Bang Bang Homestea d (140 .39E
     18.32S) at approximately 0~~5 houro on 14th September.    Theee
     obserYations were followed b~ a loud explosion.
               The light flash !rom the object and the noise from t he
     explosion were sufficient to wake people at several homesteads as far
     as thirty-five miles apart.
                Full details, including the direction of travel of the object
     have not ~et been received here.    However, questionnaires have been
     forwarded to Cloncurey Police Station !or cootpletion by the vari ous
I    witnesses.
               Two observers at Bang Bang Homestead reported hearing more
     explosions at appr.ximately 2145 hours on 14th September, but reports
     of th~exploaions have not been receiTed from other sources.
               I would appreciate 10u~forwarding anJ information you ma1
     hold which may be of uae in eTaluating the cause of t hese phenomena.
                                                            Yours fa i thtully,

             Kr. X:urwood ot the :Balloon La.unohing Project, Kildura, has volunteered
              the following information:

                             "A balloon was launched from lt1.ldura on 25th August to gather
                             upper air data up to 125000 teet.     The balloon was equipped
                             with a radar reflector and systems package which were designed
                             to be jettisoned after the teats were completed, at the same
                             ttme the balloon should have self destructed.     The balloon
                             was successfully tracked by the Sydney and Williamtown radars
                             during it a flight" •

              then sighted visuilli in South East QJ.eenaland. as it continued its easter-
              ly drift.   lio radar confirmation of this aaS\UJiption can be given as the
              Charleville radar was unserviceable and the :Brisbane radar was tracking a

..,

             One Sunday night travelling back to Mel bourne from Warrnambool with three other

      people we saw some strange lights in the sky. We were about one mile past PANliURE Bail
      when we first si~ed it. In the distance comming over the tree tops (30-40 feet above
      the tree tops) we could see a red light flasing, comming closer.     At first I thought
      it must be a plane crashing, but as it came closer we noticed it was a cluster of
      four or five red lights and one pale yellow lignts flashing on and off.     I shouldn't
      really say flashing on ~ off; because all though they were flashing they remained visible
      all the time.    We could not see any shape just lights, even though it was quite close,
      It kept at the same hight all the time and moved very slowly (it reminded me of a bird
      mx hovering while lmoking for something to eat).     We stopped the car and opened the
      windows but could not hear any sound, this may be due to the fact tha t it was very
      windy.    The driver turned the car around and drove back to Pannure, we could still
      see it at various stages ~iiia«xfwxtkwrx..-~ when the trees f did not get in the way.
      The driver went into a house at PANMURE and told the owner what we had seen.     To our
      surprise the owner said he had seen the same lights on two different occassions.
      This remin4ed me off an article in the Warrnambool "Standard" (newspaper) of a woman
      who said that ..X when she was driving to K Terang (26 miles from Warrnsmbool) she
      saw some strange lights that seemed to be hovering in the air,following her in the
      car.
             Well all I can say," the four of us in that car positively know that those lights
      were not an aeroplane or helecopter.    I think the beet thing you could do is make
      some enquir6ee with the IXXIKE PANMURE People.

                                INVESTIGATION OF UFO SIGHTING


                  Attached are two reports of an unusual aerial
        sighting in the Bairnsdale/Sale area, which has been
        submitted to this Headquarters by the Investigating Officer
        of the incident at RAAF East Sale .



                                                           L_J
                                                         (T . S. FAIRBAIRN)
                                                         Wing Commander
                                                 For Air Officer Commanding
       Encl:
                       2 reports with        (
                       unit evaluation
                               DIT'RODUCTION


1.         At about mid.nieht on ..~.:uesday 17 Jw1 69 !Jr . Gary ~EcPhillips
and ~ . Milton Hitchins reported siehting an unidentified Flying Object
to Sale rolice . In their report they alleee that a bright li~ht in the
 sky paced their car from a :t:osi tion on the Princes highv1ay just west
of the Bairnsdale Fruit Fly Road Block to a position just south of
Stratford.
2.        Constable Sherrit who heard their report has stated that
they were botn sober and apparently extret;lely frightened by their
experience . Tnis coupled with the rattler unspectacular nature of the
sighting suevests tnat the report is genuine .


                        QijaLITY OF EVIDE CE

3.        'l'he quality of information available from the witnesses was
poor . ~ach was interviewed separately and althoueh their stories were
generally similar, one r..arked discrepancy relating to the colour of the
light could not be resolved.
4.          Although both witnesses stated that the light moved with the
car , I e;ained the impression that it was a stationary lieht some distance
away, ..- the apparent motion with the car being illusory and due to
relative motion between the car the trees adjacent to the road .

5.         The witnesses descriptions of the rBnner in which the lieht
skirted ~tratford were vague , though I ._.athered that it diminished in size
durinv this time , increasing again beyond Stratford. This effect could
nave been due to the comparative intensity of the town ' s ntreet lights
and 1 ernaps tne security of the town subjectively affectinu their judg~ ­
ment . ~1e light may have manouevered around the town but it cannot be
discounted that an apparen t chanee in brillance was mistaken for
relative notion .

                             POS.:>IBLE


 .} .      A number of man- made and natural phenomena were considered in
 ae~ ch of a satisfactory explanation .

            (a))   Aircraft . ~here were no known movements of civil or
                   nrilitary aircraft• in the vicinity .

            (bJ    Visible Satellites . Reference to the Visible Satellites
                   Basses Bulletin shows that no satellites would have been
                   visible at that time . In any case the duration of the
                   sighting would have ruled out a satellite .

            ( c)   L.eteoroloP'ical Balloon . A balloon was released from RAAF
                   East Sale at 1015Z three hours before the sighting, and
                   it is est imated that it would not have been visible for
                   more than 30 Dinutes . In addition, the prevailing wind
                   would have carried it to the south and east of the pos-
                   ition .




                                                      /(e)   Local
                                       - 2-

          (e)       Local Phenomena . The position of the Esso plant waste
                    gas burner is well to the south of the observed light .
           (f)      Reflections.   Reflections from internal lights on the
                    car windscreen are ruleq out because t'H, lieht was
                    observed from outside the car as well .

              (g)   Meteorite or e entry of Orbital Vebris.    The duration of
                    the sighting rules this out .

              (r)   Celestt ial Bodies . I~rs and Jupiter were the only sign-
                    ificant bodi~s visible. l~rs 1 position was in-
                    consistent with the o servation, ho~ever, Jupiter
                    ~as bearing 286° T and elevation 1~ - within the
                    l~ts of tne object ' s estimated position.


              (j)   Heteorolo ical Conditions . At the time of the sighting
                    a !;larked inve1.·sion layer existed. Such surfaces are
                    known to have strollb reflective properties . This offers
                    a possible explanation .




7.         It is the op~nl.on of the Investieation Officer that the report
was genuine and that the light observed was the planet Jupiter, the size
and brilliance distorted by atmospheric tlffects and/or imagiunt~on .
                                        bte"
          Alternatively it could haveAa manifestation of some other
na+ ral phenomenon sufficiently rare as to be beyond the experience
of the observers , for exaople, liuht reflected frou an inversion surface .

9.            The exact nature could not be determined fro    the info~ation
available .
